Austria thus has a much stricter banking secret than Germany or almost all the other Member States of the European Union. The Austrian banking secret is regulated by law in the BWG and also protected by criminal law. I therefore believe that we can continue to maintain the position that we have taken so far, namely the anonymity of savings books and savings accounts due to the measures already taken, with regard to the European Union.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00082_d7e4358	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00082_d7e4373	Ladies and gentlemen! This is, as I believe, a very important issue that concerns the whole of the European Union. I see this less from the possibilities of tax revenue, but I see the need for harmonisation, particularly in the capital taxation sector within the European Union, as such as a priority, in order to eliminate unfair competition arising from the creation of so-called tax havens. I have therefore recorded as very positive that the Luxembourg Presidency, in its work programme presented on Monday at the Ecofin Council in Brussels, has made the harmonisation of taxes an important issue, not only in those areas which are provided for in the Monti report, i.e. also taking account of the distortion of competition in various areas. I am aware, however, that the harmonisation of the European tax system is an extremely difficult matter because the tax systems of the individual European countries have an individual history and in each country the focus is in another area. I would like to demonstrate this by an example in order to get away from capital taxation. As far as VAT is concerned, for example, in the 15 countries of the European Union, we have a difference of 10 percentage points, i.e. 15 percent in the country with the lowest value added tax and 25 percent in that with the highest value added tax. An approach to these systems is, of course, extremely difficult. There are problems on the one hand for those who have to reduce 25 percent and have to reduce 2 or 3 percentage points. As an Austrian, we could now say: Wonderful, we are in the middle, we have no need! – But on the other hand, it brings many problems to a nation state if it has to explain to its people: I now have to increase VAT by 3 or 4 percentage points because of the European Union. This is a difficult chapter, which is also true in other areas, but I believe that the question of tax harmonisation is very important. I have also discussed with the representatives of those countries with which we will be in the troika, namely, with the Germans and the Finns, the question of European taxation, because I believe that this is a medium-term programme.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00082_d7e5496	Federal Minister, please.

Also, measures of the sale of loans to the Water Economic Fund, which have been relatively well run, will help to ensure that in the following years, public debt, which currently accounts for just over 70 percent, will fall to about 67 percent in the next year, to 66, 65 percent. In order to achieve this criterion, not the 60 percent is crucial – nor can it explain to a people's economy why it should be 60 percent; so it is – but it is about moving towards this 60 percent. Here in the House, I say very consciously the following – it is not true with the opinion of all EU countries –: I would say that we should not seek a zero deficit in the medium term either, because this would lead to such a restrictive policy that we would have extreme problems, particularly in the social field. In the medium term, however, we should apply the much more reasonable formula in the economic field that the deficit should not exceed GDP growth. I believe that this is a more reasonable consolidation course, which, in particular, does not limit the scope for action of the public budget to a long time.

As a Member of Parliament, who has already participated in the Committee on Genetics, I can only confirm that health protection, consumer protection and consumer protection, have always been in the foreground of consumer protection, without our desire to be anti-researchist. I would like to deal briefly with the three points of the genetic engineering people's desire. First point: No food from the gene lab. – More than 80 percent of the rejection we have to take seriously, I believe. We must also be aware, however, that the EU is a free, borderless market and that there can be no outlets. However, I do not think that the EU's novel food regulation is sufficient. But there is also a rethinking already noticeable. Nevertheless, the solution can only be called for us in Austria: I would like to thank the rapporteur, Mr President, for his excellent report. In this context, I would like to discuss here the ban on Genmais imports and a possible import ban for Genraps, which Mrs Minister has considered. I think that even if this were not to last, we would have something like a breathing break anyway. If then the complete labelling is really regulated, then this looks much different for Austrian consumers and then we have much more safety. With regard to the second point of genetic engineering, the release of genetically modified organisms in Austria, I would like to say: We must always distinguish between release for research and sowing for commercial purposes; I have already said this here in the House several times. We are not aware of the risks and dangers of such release attempts, and if we are not researched, we will never be able to determine which ones exist. However, we cannot handle the strict control yet very well. In addition, there is still no solution to the liability rules in Austria that we would all wish for. I see Mrs Prammer's call for voluntary remission of releases as quite positive, because I believe that we must deal very sensitively with what the large majority of the Austrian population wants. That must be our concern. We must also remember, however, that there is a really large number of releases in the EU area. As a Salzburger, I think that we are on the verge of being released from Bavaria. I cannot personally estimate the risks to the population, but I think it is unfair for the Salzburg head of state to refer to a zone free of genetic engineering in the light of this situation. The argument for release tests always requires the excuse to achieve a quality improvement in the interests of consumers. To this end, I must say: I simply believe that this is a distortion of actual action. As far as I know, only one percent of release attempts are being made to improve quality. Perhaps you can meet all of this with a stronger promotion of real organic farming. But I am not very confident when I look at the approach to the seed law. We would very much have liked to lay down the labelling requirement. However, there is only one motion for a resolution that remains because our coalition partner has left us in the rain. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Schmidt.]] Very right! Third point: No patent on life. – I can fully agree to this request. In my opinion, one can only patent inventions anyway, but not discoveries. There is a proposal for a European Commission directive on legal protection of biotechnological inventions and on this proposal already 64 amendments. The next reading will take place in the European Parliament and the Council will have to deal with it. On the occasion of accession to the European Union, we have negotiated a right of participation for the main committee in very important legislative matters, and I would like to make a great effort to see the main committee deal intensively with this so-called patent directive in the coming autumn in order to support the competent minister, equipped with appropriate orders, in Brussels so that he can really do so in the interests of the Austrian people. I believe that 1 225 790 entries to this genetic engineering popular desire are more than to be taken seriously. But we must also be so honest as to tell people what room we have, what is feasible for us and what we cannot implement. Honesty, I believe, is the supreme command in such a sensitive matter as genetic engineering, where we all cannot estimate what will happen to us, as intensively as we may have been dealing with this matter.

While cleaning and providing assistance are also areas in which women are active, there is not our focus, but my emphasis is on "qualified women's jobs". The demand for women's people's desire for a right to part-time work for parents with infants remains on the table. In any case, we social-democratic women support them, because this would offer both parents the opportunity to reconcile work and children. He conceals the true intentions behind it. For if, on the one hand, services available today were to be taken back, shortened or no longer given and the childcare facilities would become expensive, but on the other hand, women would then be given a check to ensure their childcare, then that is certainly not the way in which we imagine being able to reconcile work and children. How about a health check? – Then every person with the check determines whether they operate on the almonds themselves or seek help? Or what about a "webauscheck"? – Does each person himself then determine whether they are making or making their own way? That's not how it can be! The public is responsible and has to provide for childcare, and we also take action to do so. I would like to make one last point, namely, pensions. There are also different views in this regard. We are in favour of an independent pension scheme for all women. We think it is useful to stay in the system and we are in favour of expanding compulsory insurance in such a way that it corresponds to the reality of life of women. That is to say, an essential element should and would not be – I can't do this now – that child care times are better taken into account and therefore all women have an independent pension scheme and are insured at age and not dependent.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00082_d7e13062	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! Allow me to begin by saying in a few words that the Federal Chancellor cannot take part in today's debate on the urgent question of the Freedom Party and must use his constitutional representation. This is all the more so than his current stay in Salzburg at the "Central and Eastern European Economic Summit" is closely related to the subject-matter you choose. The summit in Salzburg today is probably one of the largest and highest-ranking political and economic dialogues with the countries of Central and Eastern Europe, which is also underlined by the presence of top-ranking representatives of Eastern European states and the host country of Austria. It is therefore an important event for the Federal Government's foreign and economic policy initiatives. [[Abg. Ing. Reichhold: Can you take Haigermoser's speech a little bit?]] High house! I should like to refer briefly to what Mr Edlinger, the Federal Minister of Economic Affairs, said yesterday, explained on the economic report. [[Abg. Ing. Reichhold: Can you respond to our speech? Then we'll save time!]] The Austrian economic research institutes, the European Commission, the OECD and the International Monetary Fund are expected to grow by around 1.5 percent. For 1998, economic researchers even expect growth of about 2.2 percent. The situation on the labour market remains favourable in international comparison. An unemployment rate of 4.2 percent is forecast for 1997, which is well below the EU average of 10.5%. Despite this situation favourable in international comparison, the fight against unemployment for the Austrian Federal Government has absolute priority. Our stated objective is to restore full employment in Austria. In recent months the Federal Government has decided on many important projects. I refer as examples to the export and technology offensive as well as to the apprenticeship package. All these initiatives will strengthen Austria's economic location and help create and safeguard jobs. I answer your individual questions as follows: Question 1: The objective of the export offensive of the Austrian Federal Government is very high. These are new priorities in export financing, the extension of the financial instrument, the provision of new services, especially for small and medium-sized enterprises, joint marketing of existing funding facilities, simplification of administrative procedures, improvement of coordination and cooperation in export promotion. These ambitious objectives require an in-depth analysis of the situation. The "Export offensive" working group led by Dipl.-Ing. Pühringer and Dr. Dernoscheg have devoted themselves intensively to this task and informed the Federal Government in Rust about the Ist-Analysis and the Action Catalogue. In the coming weeks and months, intensive work will be carried out on the implementation of the proposals for measures. Especially in the areas of promoting exports and promoting research and development, we must permanently question our instruments for efficiency and effectiveness. It is political to ask the opposite question, as Austria, today one of the richest countries, could have achieved such a high economic standard without the export offensives made in previous years. This federal government will not go the way to ignore critical analyses. On the other hand, it will take the present proposals to make a further massive advance towards increasing exports and increasing research and development in our country. Questions 2 and 3: I cannot see that Austria has slid down on the scientifically recognised figures of prosperity per capita, unemployment, price stability and public deficits. On the contrary: We are in the forefront of the European Union with all these values and have further improved in 1996/97. Other key figures may have a right for specific areas, but the Federal Government is primarily concerned about the economic situation of Austrian citizens. Austria is still in the leading field. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] This does not mean, however, that we can reject it. On the contrary: As stated in the Government Declaration, the Federal Government has the firm will, together with the Austrians, to use and actively shape our opportunities for the future. Question 4: The OECD report does not contain a passage that would have meant that structural reforms would be overdue. Rather, it notes that Austria has responded to the main challenges, such as budget consolidation, public service reforms, privatisation, health care, education. In order to resolve the problem of pensions, which is also mentioned in the report, I would just like to refer to the ongoing debate initiated by Mrs Social Minister Hostasch. Of course, the report also contains further suggestions, which the Federal Government continues to examine. Furthermore, I fully share the OECD's assessment that Austria will face challenges in the future. I would also like to say here that the OECD report explicitly states that full employment for Austria is not an illusion but an attainable goal. Question 5: First of all, I would like to say that the great problem of fiscal consolidation is currently being dealt with successfully. This also shows the EU Commission's calculations that the government deficit adjusted for the economy in 1997 will be only 2.5 percent of gross domestic product, lower than the actual deficit of 3 percent. As an accelerated economic upturn is forecast for 1998/99, the analysis confirms that the actual deficit will be safe under the 3-percent mark. Moreover, how much percentage of the real deficit is economic, is entirely in addition to the question of how high the deficit is. On question 6: The current account deficit, not the trade deficit, is the decisive factor in economic policy. This will amount to about 44 billion schilling in 1997, according to the current Wifo forecast in 1997. Empirical studies of the Federal Ministry of Finance do not find any negative impact on the current macroeconomic conditions, which can influence Austrian economic policy, on the current account. Rather, it should be a problem caused by exogenous factors in the area of tourism. The current current current current account deficit is too high in the medium and long term – not because we fear Austria's inability to pay, but because the prosperity in Austria must be safeguarded. Therefore, the Federal Government has initiated the export offensive known to you. Question 7: The cause of the trend towards a reduction in tourism demand is mainly due to the accelerated decline in the German market segment in the summer season. This is facing an increasing interest of the rest of foreign guests through the compensation of structural adjustments in the tourism sector in favour of the elevated segment. [[Abg. Haigermoser: Who wrote down all this?]] [[Abg. May. This by first of all: Changes in higher education service law – departure from the principle of service invention – second: Establishment of a patent credit promotion action within the framework of the Innovation Agency, third: Facilitating procedural provisions when filing patents with the Austrian Patent Office. In the course of the reorganisation of Austrian technology policy, the establishment of a patent collecting agency is intended with the aim of ensuring the optimal marketing of research results from university and non-university research institutions and the provision of services through the results of company-related research and development. Therefore, if there is a problem with the patent and current account balances, this is due to the overall low research and development expenditure of Austria. Haigermoser: The word is true!]] On the question 16: This analysis is mainly determined by structural causes. In particular, the question of enterprise sizes is to be addressed. Only a few domestic companies are able, because of their size, to take over the role of research-intensive management companies for whole regions, such as Switzerland and Sweden. On the other hand, the Austrian economy accounts for around 95 percent of small and medium-sized enterprises. At present, the Austrian Federal Government is setting up a series of initiatives to significantly increase the research intensity of small and medium-sized enterprises and their ability to absorb new technologies. It should also be noted that the Federal Government, in its clause in Rust, did not only take note of the analysis, but also decided to implement the resulting reform proposals of the Technology Representatives. On the question 17: The international re-indebtedness agreements aim at making claims in the long term and minimizing damage. The re-indebtedness agreements concluded within the framework of the international community follow the model of coordinating creditors worldwide and thus excluding the disadvantage of individual creditors. Austria has always shouldered its responsibility in this area with great commitment and accuracy, thus representing the interests of taxpayers. Overall, debt re-indebted loans, as you did correctly, with 103 billion schilling out. Austria has been taking part in the Paris Club meetings since 1977. The aim is to facilitate debt. Two options are offered at international level: first, the cancellation of debt, second reduction in interest rates. Austria has opted for a reduction in interest rates from budgetary considerations. The aim of the re-debt contracts is to make claims in the long term. So far, there have been cumulative repayments from the debtor countries of around 35 billion schillings in capital and interest. The Federal Government will continue the path agreed in the Paris Club. The international debt strategies and debt initiatives are aimed at re-directing the countries concerned into orderly economic lines. On the question 18: The Austrian rules on foreign shares in export transactions are in line with the European Union's rules in compliance with the OECD Consensus. We are moving in the field of tension as high domestic shares as possible on the one hand as well as as as good as possible support for the Austrian export industry on the other. It is about the competitiveness of the Austrian economy, which achieves its success in a globalised economy and also with foreign shares. On question 19: The measures recently adopted in the field of foreign employment do not provide for any relief in re-admission of foreign labour to the Austrian labour market. Foreign employment is still regulated by quota restrictions and a strict labour market test in each individual case of authorisation. Priority in the labour market is given to the re-employment of the identified unemployed. The employability of the labour market is expected to be limited in the next few years, so that a further increase in the foreign labour force potential needs to be maintained. The new admission of foreign workers will therefore be limited in the next few years to the level of managers needed to safeguard jobs. However, those foreign citizens who have been in Austria regularly for many years are expected to receive employment opportunities. However, a strict labour market audit will ensure that the labour market integration of these persons takes place in a labour market-friendly way. The main objectives for the foreign employment policy of the coming years are: Limitation of the re-entry of workers, integration of the long-standing and optimum utilization of existing labour potential in all sectors of employment. Questions 20 and 21 There are currently no concrete intentions to promote tax promotion for foreign establishments and subsidiaries. Nor is there any concrete intention at present to create an accounting presentation of foreign source tax. Both proposals are long-term demands of the economy, which were also discussed within the framework of the export offensive working group. The field of indirect support has been passed on to a working committee of the Tax Reform Commission for further examination. On the question 22: In the field of Austrian export promotion procedures, the institutions concerned, in particular the Austrian Control Bank, must be regarded as particularly efficient. They provide sufficient services for the Austrian export industry. In addition to the control bank, the Austrian export promotion system is also carried by the service facilities and foreign trade agencies of the Chamber of Commerce. The East-West Fund is also important in the area of liability. Within the framework of the German Federal Government's export offensive, a number of measures will be put in place to make the Austrian financing and support system even more performance-oriented as a whole. On the question 23: The funds available to the Austrian Chamber of Commerce for External Economic Development have fallen from 2.4 billion schilling to 1.6 billion as the export promotion contribution has been lost as a result of the accession of the EU. This drop by one third has necessarily had to lead to reductions in the funding area. The funds available will continue to be used by the Economic and Social Committee for the maintenance of the external economic organisation and the support of the export industry. Since the conversion of the financing, the figures of the pension obligations of the Chamber of Commerce have no longer been borne by the budget, but are financed by pension provisions which the Austrian Chamber of Commerce has paid in the sense of an ordinary merchant in order to be able to meet future payment obligations under this title. [[Abg. Haigermoser: That's where you have to look at the control report, dear friend! – That was not his friend who wrote it to him!]] Question 24: The proportional division of functions is not intended. On question 25: The institutional involvement of employers and workers in key areas of economic policy is, of course, a proven principle, which should not be completely eliminated in the future. Furthermore, I refer to the answer to question 24. On the question 26: The five points for clarity, openness and control are addressed to those companies in which the federal, state and local authorities have a decisive influence. The Federal Government is concerned with the transparent and objective selection of executives. The public procurement of board positions is an important element of these five points. Specific to the Austrian National bank: According to the current law, the appointment of the directors of the Austrian National Bank is solely the responsibility of the General Council of the Austrian National Bank. The appointment of the new members of the Board of Directors on 26 June 1982, following a public invitation to tender required in accordance with the 1982 tendering law, is the subject of the appointment of the new members of the Board of Directors. Adopted unanimously by the General Council on June 1997. This procedure ensures an objective selection. The Executive Board of the P.S.K. AG has also been tendered. The decision on the composition of the Executive Board has been made by the Supervisory Board of the P.S.K. AG. The decisions of the Executive Board shall be taken by the institutions of the company. The Board order at ÖBB was made by the Supervisory Board after a call for tender. ÖKB and CA are companies which are not subject to the decisive influence of the public sector. The orders are therefore not covered by the provision of the tender law. On questions 27 and 28: Austrian exporters receive guarantees for export transactions and, if necessary, a refinancing of their home banks. There can be no question of nationalising the risk. [[Abg. Haigermoser: This is strong tobak!]] In addition, the Austrian export promotion process is an extremely effective and competitive instrument that allows many exports to be exported. On question 29: A warranty fee must be paid for the acquisition of liabilities, whether the guaranteeee is an Austrian exporter or a bank. There is no way to speak of free movement. Question 30: The development of interest rates also in the Austrian export promotion process depends on multiple national and international factors which are not in the influence of the Austrian Federal Government. On question 31: Under the Export Promotion Act 1981 as in force, any trader who fulfils the legal requirements may apply for a guarantee. The key component of the catalogue of measures of the Export offensive Working Group is also the improvement of advisory services for small and medium-sized enterprises. This will make it possible to expand the circle of companies supported. An extension of the use of export promotion will ultimately also depend on the commitment and risk-taking of Austrian companies, which successfully prevail on foreign markets. I hope to have answered your questions in detail. I was not willing to respond to any calls because it is my job to answer the questions raised correctly and in the interests of the Federal Government.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00082_d7e16763	Mr President. High house! The Austrian National Council today takes a legal decision of no doubt extraordinary importance. This has, last but not least, brought the public discussion of the past few weeks into general consciousness. Some observers may be surprised at this, but it is not a question of large-scale public action, not a systematic restructuring of a criminal legal area, but, particularly in the most controversial questions of the so-called large noise attack and of the grid investigation of narrow margin areas, of exception of police investigation work in the service of criminal justice. If closer attention is given, of course, to the high level of assessment of the forthcoming decision, it is indeed very fundamental questions of maximum symbolic power. On the one hand, it is one of the possible answers to the very serious challenge of the state and society by organised, often cross-border crime and the provision of the law enforcement authorities with state-of-the-art technology, on the other hand, it is about the facilitation of deep intervention of state power into privacy and, on the other hand, of secret – also associated with, so to speak, official deception – instead of open investigation. That means, ladies and gentlemen, the conflict of principles and values is programmed, the balance of interests is delicate and the decision is certainly difficult. That is why I believe that there are also various considerations, weightings and views of democratic respect on these issues. No one of us can be wrong here. Before the experience of my own formation of opinions, I believe that many of you will also make an 'inner majority decision'. I would like to take advantage of the fact that from the outset, in the discussion that has been held more than two years ago and which has not been overhaste, I have pointed to the difficult path between the pursuit of police investigation efficiency on the one hand and the most far-reaching respect for the fundamental and personality rights of the individual on the other. While this balance is characterised by the right to criminal proceedings as a whole, it has also been referred to as the applicable constitutional law, in the monitoring of citizens with technical means, this weighting has increased sharply, and in the offices and therapeutic areas of the professions with particular duty of silence, this increase reaches its extreme culling. I believe that this has become very aware of all of us in the course of the discussion, even if, in the meantime, what is probably known, is not the target person seeking such a trust, even if he is capable of doing so, but only the suspect professional secretaries themselves, and also the latter only under special conditions and with the express authorisation of the legal protection officer. Fortunately, then, this point is not about the daily life of criminal justice, but, if at all, about the extreme exception, so, as I mean, more about theory than about practice. We should not lose sight of this, nor should we lose sight of the fact that there are no noise-free zones in the mentioned species in comparable European regulations. The Federal Ministry of Justice, as you know, ladies and gentlemen, was not a pioneer of the new investigation methods. However, we have never removed the constructive discussion and have also focused our attention on the borders and on the regulations and practices of other legal systems. Thus we have taken up the reality of our time and at the same time our legal responsibility from the beginning. I am convinced that the Ministry of Justice's regulatory approach to optical and acoustic surveillance, to the automation-aided data balance and to the small instrument scheme we developed in late summer 1995 and to the government template in January 1996 have passed the test samples of a wide variety of discussions. It has also found international recognition. However, the following discussions also showed that further improvements and fine-tuning were possible, as well as additional improvements in the protection of rights and the fight against abuse. This is not least the merit of critical and skeptical votes, as well as the result of in-depth, often difficult discussions at the parliamentary level. That deserves full recognition. Ladies and gentlemen! The result presented in the form of a comprehensive amendment proposal reflects the ringing around balanced and at the same time innovative solutions. As always, every individual decides in principle, I can assure you: The solutions found, in particular the closely defined admissibility requirements, the procedural safeguards that ensure mutual control of police, prosecutors and court, and an accompanying examination by the legal protection officer, and the multi-stage measures against any possible abuse form a high-level network. There was a remarkable new country, for example, as the most striking examples, in the creation of the independent legal protection officer as a new procedural party or in the extension of the ban on evidence to civil and administrative procedures. In order to counter any abuse of the purpose of alienating sensitive surveillance results, we have also provided for new intelligence protection measures both in the field of security and judicial authorities. It is therefore not true that we have postponed responsibility for inadmissible publications in this context to the media. Instead, the criminally proven ban on publication and the right to replace the media constitute merely accompanying measures. However, if we take seriously the protection of privacy and the principle of fair procedure in connection with such profound fundamental rights intervention, we cannot make any illegal publications in the media in this area. That is what we owe to all those concerned, to those who are not involved, such as those who are guilty of crime. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] High house! With today's decision-making, the discussion, although not the legal complex of the new investigational methods as a whole, finds its preliminary conclusion to their legislative design. We are entering a new phase. From today on, the focus is no longer on the increase in the basics, but on practical implementation and critical observation. In view of the limited time-limit for the implementation of the special investigation measures, a new level is being drawn up in the field of the evaluation of practical application. This is also an opportunity to regain more common ground despite different basic settings. The Federal Ministry of Justice will contribute to this effort. As in the past, we will cooperate with the Federal Ministry of the Interior. This cooperation between the domestic and judicial authorities is, as I would like to see on this occasion, an objective and fruitful one, even though we do not fully agree on all matters from the outset and set different accents. However, the constructive spirit of this cooperation is also a good omen for the common task ahead of us of a comprehensive redesign of the criminal process. This is a project which we will now, following the conclusion of the preferred, time- and power-consuming regulation of the new methods of investigation and after a draft assessment of the out-of-court settlement and other forms of the division, push forward. I hope that it will be possible to put a draft to the public at the end of the turn of the year and complete the work during this legislative period, in particular to compensate for a deficit in the current criminal proceedings for decades and to establish up-to-date legal bases for the activities of the security authorities in the service of criminal justice. Effective criminal police work is to be made possible under the legal framework, including protection of defence rights and adequate legal protection. The use of new methods of investigation makes the legal urgency of this major project clear. – Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00082_d7e18457	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! There has been a very long, extensive and fair discussion in the Socialist Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and European Democrats on the new arrest methods. We have held this discussion with great seriousness and commitment, and I would like to use for all of us – for those who advocated it, the opponents and also for those who have been convinced – that we have discussed this issue with great sense of responsibility and very serious seriousness. After a long discussion and mature consideration, 14 members of our club have decided not to vote in favour of this draft law. We are concerned about these new arrest methods. However, the vast majority of us will not vote against it, but will leave the room when voting. The reason for this is that we fully recognise the efforts to improve. The negotiations in the Subcommittee have tried to take into account the concerns we expressed in the club and to establish mechanisms to limit and control this intervention in fundamental rights. However, it remains fundamental rights to intervene, and it is our duty to consider whether the creation of such an intervention is justified or not. In a democratic rule of law, such as Austria, we must create a very high standard and examine very carefully what methods and means are necessary and acceptable for the effective fight against crime. It is repeatedly stressed that the noise attack will only be applied in a few cases, and international examples also show that this is not a method that can be applied very frequently. I wonder whether this search method, which is to be introduced today, actually leads to the result that their supporters want. I am afraid that organised crime – and we all want to fight organized crime – will be well prepared and can in fact best resist these methods. The question for us, therefore, is whether this interference in privacy is allowed to be heard by the fact that everything that is discussed in the most private and intimate context and in the most intimate situations is not allowed to exceed a limit. For example, the fact that we can also be heard in the course of therapy seems to have been overburdened. We are also concerned about the Rasterfachung. In both cases, the fundamental rights of – we need to be aware of – completely undivided and unspoiled people are taken into account. They are thus drawn into a procedure and may not be able to resist it, because they do not even know it. One cannot rule out the possibility of error – not at the best of will – and I fear that it cannot be ruled out either that there can be a misuse of so-called interesting data. I have already expressed the concerns raised earlier, in 1993 and 1994, when the debate on the noise attack began. As I said, I recognize the efforts made to introduce these methods in a legal form, but believe that these rules are in themselves problematic. I would like to mention positively that this scheme will only be introduced temporarily. That would actually have been appropriate. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] I believe that with many of the economic laws that still apply to provisions or laws of the last century, one can no longer find the way out at the threshold of the next century. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! In the first place, I would like to make a few positive comments on a part which I am very interested in, namely, the Company Reorganisation Act. This company reorganisation law is figuratively a law that opens up the possibility for a company to visit a specialist, to make an injunction into a hospital and to try with a reorganisation plan, with a reorganisation auditor, to get its company fit again. The bankruptcy order, the forced settlement and then the liquidation, is nothing but the order for a funeral of a company. That is to say, in this government template, there are two very different legal bases according to the meaning on which, on the one hand, adaptations of the bankruptcy, compensation and the challenge order to which I shall come even closer to speak, on the other hand, an actual innovation in which the legislator must ask: Will this be the business, will the entrepreneur also accept it? We have some concerns, Mr Federal Minister for Justice, if the government template is decided without amendment – I would like to introduce it now, and it will also be distributed, and I will refer to it briefly. On the one hand, this is a central problem: An entrepreneur goes to the reorganisation, creates a reorganisation plan and then asks: What effect does this have on my business? – I get a debt reduction from, for example, a million schillings that I urgently need in order to be able to clean up. I do a reorganisation of my administration, I do a new management, I'm looking for new products. - All of this is in there. When he implements the reorganisation plan, the Federal Minister for Finance – in application of the Income Tax and Corporate Tax Act – suddenly comes back to the Federal Minister for Finance, and takes him away from the one million he has deleted from any bank between 300 000 S and 400 000 S. – This cannot be in the sense of the inventor of this law! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] This is why every businessman will say: I do not believe that I am dealing with all the banks, that I am dealing with suppliers and then sending to the Federal Minister for Finance a tax prescription, according to which I must return to him part of this recovery profit in the form of taxes. A second point is that in the subjugation of this company reorganisation law, it should be a law such as Chapter 11 in the USA. When you look at Chapter 11, you notice: There, insolvency and enforcement protection is guaranteed during the duration of this business reorganisation. . We have also incorporated a passage in this amendment which we believe is urgently necessary. We have incorporated in this amendment a paragraph which provides for a new provision in the compensation regulation: "The execution of the premises shall be carried out at the request of the depositor during the compensatory procedure after examination by the execution court if the interest on the stock is not paid in good time and in full after the opening of the compensatory procedure." It is a question of the fact that, in the compensatory procedure, we also want to have a protection of the owner that no rent is ever paid, that we rent a property, that we don't pay this rent in the compensation procedure, and that it does not damage the owner. With these three objectives, which we have formulated in this amendment, I believe that the law on adoption will be a suitable instrument in order not to let the company reorganisation become a dead one. This is a reasonable offer for the economy, and I would like to express my thanks to the Federal Minister for Justice, who has explained to us in advance with many officials what philosophy is behind this company reorganisation. ParlaMint-AT_1997-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00082_d7e19755	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Insolvency laws certainly cannot prevent insolvency. However, one can try to improve the disparity that we currently have between bankruptcys, compensations and, above all, the large number of rejected bankruptcys, in order to bring about a better economic outcome. In fact, it is wasteful of popular assets, i.e. asset destruction, if only 3 percent of insolvency proceedings are compensatory procedures, i.e. companies are continued, and if far more than half of insolvency bankruptcys are rejected due to lack of mass, so there is no procedure, no judge checks whether money has not yet been put aside or would have been available for the satisfaction of creditors. That is why a law has been established that is multilayered. It essentially amends the bankruptcy rules, amends the compensation rules, and puts the dead law, the preliminary proceedings, into a new procedure, namely the Company Reorganisation Act. This Company Reorganisation Act assumes, in contrast to the preliminary procedure, that the Company is not yet insolvent, but still insolvent, but has a need for reorganisation, which is determined by means of an opinion. This opinion may come from the tax advisor, the accountant, who has to give the confirmation note for the balance sheet, but also through the revision of the cooperatives, and I would like to have made this clear here. It is then requested by the Court of First Instance to reorganise the procedure by means of a reorganisation plan. The court shall examine by the reorganisation auditor. I would also like to point out that it is the legislator's will that this reorganisation auditor has a discretionful role and should not take over the management of the company. In the event of approval of the reorganisation plan, the procedure by judicial decision shall be repealed. Mr Schreiner! I deeply regret that you have not succeeded in ensuring that this reorganisation procedure is properly ensured by means of the appropriate reorganisation measures. However, I assume that this court decision on the reorganisation plan is to be assessed in a similar way to that in court decisions in the compensatory and bankruptcy proceedings. If it turns out that this is not the case because the courts or the financial procedures come to different conclusions, then of course, in the sense of Mr Schreiner, we need action so that we do not destroy a reorganisation plan by suddenly meeting the marode company's excessive tax demands. I consider this new procedure to be an opportunity for companies, although I must admit that it is not in the Austrian entrepreneurship soul to run to court immediately in the event of difficulties in the operation. That is, if this procedure is to succeed, the pressure will have to come from outside. I expect the banks to influence companies in good time to initiate this procedure before insolvency occurs. Banks have the possibility – as well as all other creditors – that their rehabilitation measures will then be subject to a certain degree of resistance to appeal. In connection with this law, we have also standardized two resolutions. The first motion for a resolution concerns the very unsatisfactory situation so far, that measures are suddenly considered by third parties to be capital-replacent, do not fall into the bulk and are therefore not available for quotas. For these reasons, banks have so far turned to the money tap rather than to risk providing equity-replaced equity benefits. That is why we have addressed a resolution to the Federal Minister that the legal provisions on equity-replaced company benefits must be submitted in such a timely manner that they can be decided before the end of this legislative period. I hope that this will be next year, not the end of the legislative period, but the presentation of this clear definition. I am particularly concerned about insolvency as the very unsatisfying regulation on the remuneration of the mass administrators so far. It was true that, for the creditors, often not transparent enough, the remuneration of the mass administrators was made regardless of the ratio to the quota that came out. I am also of the opinion – and this has now been done by the novelle in bankruptcy law – that bankruptcy should not be delayed to time indefinite, but that the bankruptcy procedure is a winding-up procedure, which presupposes that a continuation is no longer meaningful. That is to say, a 20 per cent compensating rate cannot be achieved, and then it is to be liquidated. I am in favour of the fact that, according to their effort, mass administrators are of course rewarded accordingly. However, I also believe that it is very useful to try to protect creditors when mass administrators have a successful remuneration. It will not be to be set at 100 percent in the quota, but it should be based to a large extent on the attainable quota. I do not see why masses always produce so much income that the mass administrator's fee is covered and nothing remains in the quota for the other creditors. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] In order not to give the wrong impression, everyone has to shave over a comb: There are very serious lawyers who are trying to achieve that the holding can be continued and that something remains for the ratio of the other creditors next to the fee of the lawyers. Of course, it is also natural for the reorganisation auditor that his fees are to be seen in connection with the maritime companies, and the Minister of Justice should also make proposals for this. It is not intended to take into account, for example, securities or shares or provisions et cetera. That is to say, there has been an objective argument that has not been taken into account. ParlaMint-AT_1997-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00082_d7e19873	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Unfortunately, as the recent figures of insolvency statistics show, the trend of the increase in the number of insolvency proceedings in Austria has continued for a number of years, and the number of loss of assets is still dubiously high. The causes of this dynamic development in recent years are not rarely due to the accelerating structural changes in our economy, in the redistribution of markets and locations, ultimately in the globalisation of the world economy. Insolvency also belongs to the system-immanent selection procedure of the market economy. But it is true that economic operators always point out that a significant proportion – some even think, a major part – of the dynamic increase in the number of insolvencies in the business sector of the companies concerned, many of the insolvent companies no longer meet the business requirements, for example – by just one point – it lacks a functioning computer as a condition of permanent self-control. In view of this, legal policy is required to counteract development through appropriate legislative measures. In the area of justice, these are measures on the one hand in the field of insolvency law and on the other on the other hand in the field of company law. The main objective of these measures must be to identify a business crisis as early as possible in order to enable the reorganisation of a viable company in time, in those cases where it is insolvent, to exploit all the possibilities of reorganisation and continuation of the company, and ultimately, if reorganisation no longer proves to be economically feasible to exploit the company as best possible. These objectives have already defined a number of legislative measures in recent times and are also based on the draft law which is now before the National Council. Milestones along this path were the Accounting Act 1990, the Insolvency Change Act 1994 and the EU Company Change Act from the previous year. The present IRAG 1997 sets out another important step towards improving insolvency prophylaxis, especially to exploit the opportunities for timely and effective restructuring of insolvency-risk companies, to facilitate the continuation and reorganisation of insolvency companies and to reduce loss of bankruptcy. One of the priorities of this legislative work is the company reorganisation law, which I believe is wrongly skeptical. This is the result of extensive discussions with experts and intensive experience gained from business practitioners with insolvency and insolvency law, and it is in line with the understanding that the earlier steps taken to reorganise a company are the more promising the more successful they are. In this sense, the reorganisation process is an offer to the still solving entrepreneurs to support him in dealing with a crisis. The key figures defined in the law, which have also been very controversially discussed, are first and foremost only to be understood as a warning light, which should cause the entrepreneur to have his economic situation checked with the help of consultants. If there is a need for reorganisation due to a well-founded numbering material and an analysis based on it, and if, as part of the restructuring plan, a financial bridge or reinforcement is required in the form of an additional loan, this will be facilitated by the Company Reorganisation Act in future by the fact that the crediting bank, if there is a failure of the reorganisation and a subsequent bankruptcy, is not exposed to the risk of a challenge. This is an important legal order which, on the one hand, should contribute to greater legal certainty, but on the other, it is also considered as a very important incentive for this law to be actually adopted by practice. Ladies and gentlemen! Early recognition of a company's crisis situations and thus insolvency prophylaxis also serve a number of measures in the areas of company law, in particular the obligation to conduct an accounting and internal control system corresponding to the requirements of that company, the extension of the reporting obligation of the Executive Board and a number of changes concerning the Supervisory Board with a view to its better information and thus more effective control activities. Facilitating the continuation of an insolvency is aimed at increasing the attractiveness of the compensation procedure and at reorganising the bankruptcy procedure towards a uniform procedure. In the initial phase of the bankruptcy, the so-called test phase, the difference between compensating and recovery is to be made. It is also important to take several measures to reduce bankruptcy because of lack of mass and the future possibility of bankruptcy even with only one creditor. For example, a debtor, who has only one creditor, can obtain a residual debt exemption – a scheme that, in particular, offers assistance to spouses who are liable for a debt only and who are then asked for cash. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! Today's decision-making IRAG 1997 is certainly a milestone in the development of Austrian company insolvency rights. However, it is also part of the comprehensive efforts of the Federal Ministry of Justice to improve the legal framework for the Austrian economy and thus to strengthen Austria's economic location in the large economic area of Europe. Other ongoing legislative projects in this area concern the right of readmission, to which a draft assessment has recently been sent, cooperative law – the first step is to be sent to the National Council in the near future of the draft cooperative revision law – and the right of capital replacement to which a motion for a resolution is to be made today. The legal area of capital replacement is closely related to insolvency law, but in almost every business crisis there are also issues of equity replacement. This has already been taken into account in the Corporate Reorganisation Act. Furthermore, this problem is already being discussed in a working group of the Federal Ministry of Justice, so I am confident that, despite the difficulties of matter, a legislative decision can be reached during this legislative period. - Thank you many times.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00082_d7e19965	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The core of the insolvency law innovation is the Company Reorganisation Act. I cannot help the Minister of State if he speaks of a "milestone" in this regard, which is set by the creation of this law. From my experience in practice, I prophesy a stillbirth, a dead birth similar to that recorded at the time when the preliminary procedure was introduced. The preliminary procedure, which was regulated in the context of the compensation regulation, was a stillbirth. There have been only a few attempts to implement the preliminary procedure, and after one, two years it has completely fallen asleep. Nothing else I'm prophesying about this corporate reorganisation law. Why? - Because it doesn't matter to the company! The entrepreneur proceeds under state control, the procedure is initiated, an auditor is appointed, experts are appointed, credit protection associations, AKVs will be assuredly agreed; no confidentiality can be said at all. I am predicting that, if there are indeed attempts to implement this law, companies will not get into insolvency by the situation that their equity has fallen to 8 per cent, but only by the application. Because one thing is clear, ladies and gentlemen: It is an illusion if one assumes that this procedure can be handled under the ceiling, under the spell, without the observation of creditor protection associations. Indeed, the creditor protection associations have the obligation, in accordance with their statutes, to protect their customers, their clients from the default of claims, and I guarantee you that the so-called confidential lists of creditors' associations will, of course, show any initiations of reorganisation procedures. Ladies and gentlemen! We know, of course, from experience in practice, what this leads to. – That means no more goods deliveries against open invoices, only more train around train, that is, the goods will not be delivered without cash payment. This naturally leads to a liquidity gap. Furthermore, credit lines will no longer be extended, or – and this is also quite clear given the conditions of the banks – the initiation of a reorganisation process is a so-called important reason for credit to be due. That's one. The second is: It doesn't matter to the company. This means that the entrepreneur can't achieve anything: He has no bankruptcy protection, he has no execution protection, and he is not even left a claim or part of the claim. So what's the whole thing to do?- Oh, my God! Moreover, the obligation to initiate the reorganisation procedure is limited to large companies subject to audit. In other words, it is a matter of companies which have, at all events, a board of auditors, accountants, tax advisors and lawyers. What are they supposed to do in such a procedure? The only beneficiaries could be banks, because The purpose of this law is, as has been said, to make loans permanent. Only: This is at the expense of the other creditors. For if a bank lends a new credit line and uses for example a not yet taxed property of the company as a security and is intended to be stable, then this asset will be ensured in the event of a subsequent insolvency at the expense of all creditors and banks. That should not be the meaning of this law! Secondly, Shareholders' loans are not replaced by equity capital, i.e. in any insolvency proceedings. – This once again only protects the individual entrepreneur and not the creditors. We do something for debtors without interruption, partly certainly justified. We have to think of the creditors sometime. And if that's the right way, I really want to doubt that. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] That is why we have linked our minimum request for approval to a very objective and well-founded amendment, as I believe. This amendment requires that the objectives of Chapter 11 of the US Code of Bankruptcy are actually implemented. Because in the United States we have seen that this offers real protection. A company is under the protection of Chapter 11 for a period of time and is given a breathing break. The creditors cannot make their claims subject to a certain period of time. The purpose of this scheme is to improve the structure of the company. New business areas could be opened up, other improvements could take place. – That would make sense. The execution barrier: How to reorganise the company if deliveries take place only more train around train and there is no execution barrier? In short: This law should only enter into force if these minimum requirements are met, for only then can it prove itself. I say again: There is actually no reasonable reason for a company without applying for these improvements to the reorganisation in the sense of this law.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-07-10-020-XX-NRSITZ-00082_d7e20421	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! And that's the approach of this novel. I do not want to repeat the words of the Federal Minister, but to mention only briefly: These are, on the one hand, the rescinding of the rejections of bankruptcy due to the corresponding measures, because today, in fact, assets are often destroyed unnecessarily and creditors are damaged. Secondly, to improve the compensation procedure by making a compensation application possible in the future before insolvency has occurred. Thirdly, the bankruptcy procedure should not become a funeral à la Schreiner, but rather the bankruptcy procedure should also, in so far as the chance of reorganisation is held when the mass administrator is forced to check whether the company is worthy of rehabilitation within 90 days. If it is worthy of rehabilitation, he shall allow the restoration in the form of a forced compensation application. If this is not possible, he has to sell as a whole, and only if that is not possible, then he should liquidate in parts. Overall, it is also – as I believe – a turnaround towards the maintenance of businesses and jobs as far as possible. I know that the Best Insolvency Change Act does not completely prevent insolvency in the future. However, with a good law, we can help to protect the interests of creditors better, but perhaps also to clean up some kind of company early and thus preserve existence and jobs.

At this point, I would like to thank our Federal Minister, Eleonora Hostashda, for working with Vehemence and commitment to solving the issue of social security, even slightly employees. I am convinced that we will soon come to a positive solution on this issue. An application aimed at excluding a group that has only recently been excluded from the insurance obligation is therefore rather counterproductive. The recording of all incomes in the social security system may require new arrangements for individual areas. In this context, we will have to discuss the overall problem. However, further exclusions from the insurance obligation do not appear to be acceptable as a transitional scheme, which is why we do not accede to this request.

The whole development of this law was accompanied by some difficulties. Finally, the shadow of a tragic error fell on this bill. Ladies and gentlemen! Of course, this law cannot cover all the risks posed by the health and nursing profession. But this law provides a basis and a tool for continuing high-quality work in the hospital bed. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] In particular, the redefinition of the job descriptions and the description of the areas of activity of persons working in care professions were urgently necessary and necessary. A documentation obligation is also introduced for the first time in health and nursing professions. In other words, all the care activities that are set up and carried out must also be documented accordingly so that they can be carried out and monitored accordingly. This documentation mainly contributes to the professionalisation of our profession, which has already begun in Europe and in other countries, but now also takes place in Austria. Of course, the introduction of the documentation requirement also requires the documentation of care, and this documentation within the framework of the care process is particularly important for us. A particularly important measure to ensure quality in our health and nursing professions is the definition of different types of special training. Up to now, compulsory special training has not been provided, nor is compulsory continuing and continuing training. However, this law lays down that the service providers are obliged to control and prove that such special training has been completed. If you work in a special field or have a teaching or management job, this special training must be completed within five years. For example, the head of the care service must undergo such training and then complete with "academicly-tested head of the care service". These include special training courses such as intensive sisters, surgical sisters or dialysis sisters. In the case of special tasks in the health and nursing profession, however, it is also particularly important that there is no fragmentation. It must remain in the process of classification into special tasks, teaching and leadership tasks. This also underlines the value of these individual tasks. It is therefore not intended to provide a separate special training for each subject area, but rather to enable the individual subjects, such as the work of the endoscopy nurses, or the sisters working in the Geriatry, to complete a training course in the context of further training. Special attention will be paid to increasing the level of care in the future and increasing in cooperation with the entire medical team. In this context, it is especially necessary to benefit from the self-confidence of nurses when the importance of all nurses within the team is equal. This will be achieved by this law, and it will also make it possible to achieve greater professional satisfaction. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] For the first time in this law, we find a detailed description of the persons working in the care professions. For the first time, it distinguishes between the area of self-responsible activity, co-responsible activity and interdisciplinary activity. This interdisciplinary field of activity is particularly involved when it comes to extramural care, care in the team and cross-disciplinary opinions. The aim is that the patient should be at the centre of the team's members and thus be able to take care of them. [[Abg. Dr. Khol: We'll all need care soon if you're still talking for a long time!]] That may be! Ladies and gentlemen, Mr Khol! The name of the law should not only have symbolic power, the word "health" should, for the first time, actually be the guiding motive of action and commit to actions that promote one's own health and that of other people. Ladies and gentlemen! This law is a new foundation; those in the care professions will build upon it a house in which everyone feels well and from which everyone is strengthened. Thank you.
